For the past year and eight months, I've noticed a specific dark SUV parked outside my house at odd hours, and there are constant clicking sounds on my phone line. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I'm here because I've been feeling incredibly anxious lately. I know for a fact that a certain organization is monitoring my movements and watching everything I do. I need help figuring out how to deal with this constant surveillance.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about your mood lately, have you been experiencing any periods of depression or extreme mood swings, like feelings of euphoria or irritability?\n\nPatient: My mood has actually been pretty normal and stable, other than the anxiety from being watched. I haven't really experienced any depression or extreme mood swings. I just feel constantly on edge because of the surveillance.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any unusual perceptions, such as hearing voices or seeing things that others cannot, in addition to your belief about being surveilled?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't heard or seen anything unusual at all. My senses are completely normal, and I don't experience any hallucinations. My only concern is the fact that I am being actively monitored and watched by this group.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: Could you please clarify what you would like to know next? I want to make sure I'm answering your questions as best as I can.\n\nDoctor: How long have you believed you're being surveilled, and has this belief affected your daily life or relationships in any significant way?\n\nPatient: I have been monitored and watched by this group for about a year and eight months now. While the situation is highly stressful and forces me to take daily precautions, it hasn't really stopped me from doing my job or maintaining my relationships.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe in more detail what kind of precautions you take on a daily basis to counter or cope with the surveillance you believe you're under?\n\nPatient: I just try to do basic things to keep myself safe, but I'd rather not get into the specific details of my security routines. I'm mainly just looking for ways to manage the stress that comes with all of this.\n\nDoctor: Are you absolutely certain that you haven't experienced any brief moments of hearing or seeing things that might seem unusual to others, even if it's just occasionally or in response to your belief of being surveilled?\n\nPatient: No, I am absolutely certain that I have never seen or heard anything that isn't there. My experiences are not a matter of hearing or seeing things, but rather the reality of being monitored. I have never had any kind of visual or auditory hallucinations.\n\nDoctor: Can you help me understand what specifically leads you to believe that this organization is monitoring you, and what evidence or events have convinced you of this reality?\n\nPatient: I'm really hesitant to share the specific details or events because I'm worried about who might be listening to this conversation. The signs are completely obvious to me, but for safety reasons, I prefer not to go into the exact evidence right now.\n\nDoctor: Are you absolutely certain that you haven't experienced any brief moments of hearing or seeing things that might seem unusual to others, even if it's just occasionally or in response to your belief of being surveilled?\n\nPatient: Yes, I am completely certain about that. Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others.  Subtypes: Emotional Detachment, Social Isolation, Difficulty with Intimacy, Loss of Belonging\n[S076] Inability_Positive_Emotions: Persistent inability to experience positive emotions (e.g., inability to experience happiness, satisfaction, or loving feelings).  Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.  Subtypes: Risky Behaviors, Substance Abuse, Indisicriminate Sexual Behavior, Self-Harm\n[S079] Hypervigilance: Persistent state of being alert and tense about the surrounding environment.  Subtypes: Scanning for Danger, Safety Checking, Sustained Alertness\n[S080] Exaggerated_Startle_Response: Exaggerated startle response to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Auditory Startle, Tactile Startle, Visual Startle, Slow Recovery\n[S081] Stress_Related_Maladaptive_Response: Emotional or behavioral symptoms appearing in response to an identifiable stressor.  Subtypes: With Depressed Mood, With Anxiety, With Mixed Anxiety and Depressed Mood, With Disturbance of Conduct, With Mixed Disturbance of Emotions and Conduct, Unspecified\n[S082] Depersonalization_Derealization: Altered sense of the reality of one's self or surroundings after a traumatic event.  Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nNo, I am absolutely certain that I have never seen or heard anything that isn't there. My experiences are not a matter of hearing or seeing things, but rather the reality of being monitored.
